check\+\_\+var\+\_\+refs -\/ Check if there is a reference to any of trigger\textquotesingle{}s variables @hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} hist trigger

A trigger can define one or more variables. If any one of them is currently referenced by any other trigger, this function will determine that.

Typically used to determine whether or not a trigger can be removed
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if there are any references to a trigger\textquotesingle{}s variables, it cannot.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Return\+: True if there is a reference to any of trigger\textquotesingle{}s variables Here is the call graph for this function\+:

create\+\_\+field\+\_\+var\+\_\+hist -\/ Automatically create a histogram and var for a field @target\+\_\+hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} target hist trigger @subsys\+\_\+name\+: Optional subsystem name @event\+\_\+name\+: Optional event name @field\+\_\+name\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} name of the field (and the resulting variable)

Hist trigger actions fetch data from variables, not directly from events. However, for convenience, users are allowed to directly specify an event field in an action, which will be automatically converted into a variable on their behalf.

If a user specifies a field on an event that isn\textquotesingle{}t the event the histogram currently being defined (the target event histogram), the only way that can be accomplished is if a new hist trigger is created and the field variable defined on that.

This function creates a new histogram compatible with the target event (meaning a histogram with the same key as the target histogram), and creates a variable for the specified field, but with \textquotesingle{}synthetic\+\_\+\textquotesingle{} prepended to the variable name in order to avoid collision with normal field variables.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} variable created for the field. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily static struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structfield__var}{field\+\_\+var}}$\ast$ create\+\_\+target\+\_\+field\+\_\+var (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structhist__trigger__data}{hist\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+data}} $\ast$}]{target\+\_\+hist\+\_\+data,  }\item[{char $\ast$}]{subsys\+\_\+name,  }\item[{char $\ast$}]{event\+\_\+name,  }\item[{char $\ast$}]{var\+\_\+name }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

create\+\_\+target\+\_\+field\+\_\+var -\/ Automatically create a variable for a field @target\+\_\+hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} target hist trigger @subsys\+\_\+name\+: Optional subsystem name @event\+\_\+name\+: Optional event name @var\+\_\+name\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} name of the field (and the resulting variable)

Hist trigger actions fetch data from variables, not directly from events. However, for convenience, users are allowed to directly specify an event field in an action, which will be automatically converted into a variable on their behalf.

This function creates a field variable with the name var\+\_\+name on the hist trigger currently being defined on the target event. If subsys\+\_\+name and event\+\_\+name are specified, this function simply verifies that they do in fact match the target event subsystem and event name.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} variable created for the field. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

create\+\_\+var\+\_\+ref -\/ Create a variable reference and attach it to trigger @hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger that will be referencing the variable @var\+\_\+field\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} V\+AR field to create a reference to @system\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} optional system string @event\+\_\+name\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} optional event\+\_\+name string

Given a variable \mbox{\hyperlink{structhist__field}{hist\+\_\+field}}, create a V\+A\+R\+\_\+\+R\+EF \mbox{\hyperlink{structhist__field}{hist\+\_\+field}} that represents a reference to it.

This function also adds the reference to the trigger that now references the variable.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} V\+A\+R\+\_\+\+R\+EF field if successful, N\+U\+LL if not Here is the call graph for this function\+:

find\+\_\+any\+\_\+var\+\_\+ref -\/ Check if there is a reference to a given trigger variable @hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} hist trigger @var\+\_\+idx\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger variable identifier

Check to see whether the given variable is currently referenced by any other trigger.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger the variable is defined on is explicitly excluded -\/ the assumption being that a self-\/reference doesn\textquotesingle{}t prevent a trigger from being removed.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} V\+A\+R\+\_\+\+R\+EF field referencing the variable if so, N\+U\+LL if not Here is the call graph for this function\+:

find\+\_\+var\+\_\+ref -\/ Check if a trigger has a reference to a trigger variable @hist\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} hist trigger that might have a reference to the variable @var\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} hist trigger that owns the variable @var\+\_\+idx\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger variable identifier

Check the list of var\+\_\+refs\mbox{[}\mbox{]} on the first hist trigger to see whether any of them are references to the variable on the second trigger.

Return\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} V\+A\+R\+\_\+\+R\+EF field referencing the variable if so, N\+U\+LL if not Here is the call graph for this function\+:
